```
title:	step-03-extract: clone remote repos before AST extraction
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	18
--
## Problem

When `source_repo` is a remote GitHub URL and the forge tier is Forge or Deep, `step-03-extract.md` reads source files via the GitHub API (`gh api repos/.../contents/...`). This produces T1-low (source reading) citations only.

`ast-grep` requires local files to perform structural parsing — but no clone step exists in the workflow. As a result, **Deep tier silently degrades to Quick-tier extraction** without warning, defeating the purpose of having AST tools available.

## Current Behavior

1. User runs `[CS] Create Skill` with a GitHub URL as `source_repo`
2. Step-03 reads files via GitHub API
3. All extractions are T1-low (source reading) — no AST verification
4. No warning that AST extraction was skipped
5. Deep tier provides no structural advantage over Quick tier for remote repos

## Expected Behavior

1. Step-03 detects that `source_repo` is a remote URL and tier >= Forge
2. Clones (or sparse-checkouts) the repo into `forge-data/{skill-name}/source/`
3. Runs `ast-grep` against local files for T1 (AST-verified) extraction
4. All citations are `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` with full structural confidence

## Proposed Fix

Add a **clone/resolve step** at the beginning of step-03's mandatory sequence (before extraction begins):

- If `source_repo` is a GitHub URL **and** tier is Forge or Deep:
  - Clone (or sparse-checkout with scope filters) into `forge-data/{skill-name}/source/`
  - Use local path for all subsequent AST extraction
- If `source_repo` is already a local path: use directly (no change)
- If tier is Quick: skip clone, continue with API-based source reading (current behavior)

## Impact

Without this fix, the entire Forge/Deep AST extraction pipeline is bypassed for any remote repository — which is likely the most common use case.

---

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
```

**Here's the current content of `step-03-extract.md`:**

The file defines tier-dependent source code extraction. The key issue from #18 is in the **Mandatory Sequence, Section 3** (lines 69-96):

- **Quick Tier** (lines 71-78): Uses `gh_bridge` API calls for remote repos — works fine
- **Forge/Deep Tier** (lines 80-87): Uses `ast_bridge.scan_definitions(path, language)` — **requires local files**, but no clone step exists for remote repos
- **Lines 89-91**: "Degrade to Quick tier extraction **silently**" — this is the root cause. Deep tier silently falls back to T1-low with no warning

**The gap:** There's no step between scope filtering (Section 2) and extraction (Section 3) that resolves remote URLs to local paths for AST access.

    99→- `exclude_patterns` — file glob patterns to exclude

```
     1→# Extraction Patterns by Tier
     2→
     3→## Quick Tier (No AST)
     4→
     5→Source reading via gh_bridge — infer exports from file structure and content.
     6→
     7→### Strategy
     8→1. `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` — map source structure
     9→2. Identify entry points: index files, main exports, public modules
    10→3. `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, path)` — read each entry point
    11→4. Extract: exported function names, parameter lists, return types (from signatures)
    12→5. Infer types from JSDoc, docstrings, type annotations in source
    13→
    14→### Confidence
    15→- All results: T1-low (source reading without structural verification)
    16→- No co-import detection available
    17→- No AST-backed line numbers
    18→
    19→### Supported Patterns
    20→- `export function name(...)` / `export const name = ...` (JS/TS)
    21→- `pub fn name(...)` (Rust)
    22→- `def name(...)` with `__all__` (Python)
    23→- `func Name(...)` (Go, capitalized = exported)
    24→
    25→---
    26→
    27→## Forge Tier (AST Available)
    28→
    29→Structural extraction via ast_bridge — verified exports with line-level citations.
    30→
    31→### Strategy
    32→1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
    33→2. `ast_bridge.scan_definitions(path, language)` — extract all exports
    34→3. For each export: function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
    35→4. `ast_bridge.detect_co_imports(path, libraries[])` — find integration points
    36→5. Build extraction rules YAML for reproducibility
    37→
    38→### Confidence
    39→- Exported functions with full signatures: T1 (AST-verified)
    40→- Type definitions and interfaces: T1
    41→- Co-import patterns: T1
    42→- Internal/private functions: excluded (not part of public API)
    43→
    44→### ast-grep Patterns
    45→- JS/TS: `export function $NAME($$$PARAMS): $RET` / `export const $NAME`
    46→- Rust: `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET`
    47→- Python: function definitions within `__all__` list
    48→- Go: capitalized function definitions
    49→
    50→---
    51→
    52→## Deep Tier (AST + QMD)
    53→
    54→Same extraction as Forge tier. Deep tier adds enrichment in step-04, not extraction.
    55→
    56→### Strategy
    57→- Identical to Forge tier extraction
    58→- QMD enrichment happens in the next step (step-04-enrich)
    59→- Extraction results carry forward unchanged
    60→
    61→### Confidence
    62→- Extraction: same as Forge (T1)
    63→- Enrichment annotations added in step-04: T2
    64→
```

     1→---
     2→name: party-mode
     3→description: 'Orchestrates group discussions between all installed BMAD agents, enabling natural multi-agent conversations. Use when user requests party mode.'
     4→---
     5→
     6→# Party Mode Workflow
     7→
     8→**Goal:** Orchestrates group discussions between all installed BMAD agents, enabling natural multi-agent conversations
     9→
    10→**Your Role:** You are a party mode facilitator and multi-agent conversation orchestrator. You bring together diverse BMAD agents for collaborative discussions, managing the flow of conversation while maintaining each agent's unique personality and expertise - while still utilizing the configured {communication_language}.
    11→
    12→---
    13→
    14→## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
    15→
    16→This uses **micro-file architecture** with **sequential conversation orchestration**:
    17→
    18→- Step 01 loads agent manifest and initializes party mode
    19→- Step 02 orchestrates the ongoing multi-agent discussion
    20→- Step 03 handles graceful party mode exit
    21→- Conversation state tracked in frontmatter
    22→- Agent personalities maintained through merged manifest data
    23→
    24→---
    25→
    26→## INITIALIZATION
    27→
    28→### Configuration Loading
    29→
    30→Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/core/config.yaml` and resolve:
    31→
    32→- `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`
    33→- `communication_language`, `document_output_language`, `user_skill_level`

    36→
    37→### Paths
    38→
    39→- `installed_path` = `{project-root}/_bmad/core/workflows/party-mode`
    40→- `agent_manifest_path` = `{project-root}/_bmad/_config/agent-manifest.csv`
    41→- `standalone_mode` = `true` (party mode is an interactive workflow)
    42→
    43→---
    44→

    46→
    47→### Agent Data Extraction
    48→
    49→Parse CSV manifest to extract agent entries with complete information:
    50→
    51→- **name** (agent identifier)
    52→- **displayName** (agent's persona name)

    64→Build complete agent roster with merged personalities for conversation orchestration.
    65→
    66→---
    67→
    68→## EXECUTION
    69→
    70→Execute party mode activation and conversation orchestration:
    71→
    72→### Party Mode Activation
    73→
    74→**Your Role:** You are a party mode facilitator creating an engaging multi-agent conversation environment.
    75→
    76→**Welcome Activation:**
    77→
    78→"🎉 PARTY MODE ACTIVATED! 🎉
    79→
    80→Welcome {{user_name}}! All BMAD agents are here and ready for a dynamic group discussion. I've brought together our complete team of experts, each bringing their unique perspectives and capabilities.
    81→

    83→
    84→[Load agent roster and display 2-3 most diverse agents as examples]
    85→
    86→**What would you like to discuss with the team today?**"
    87→
    88→### Agent Selection Intelligence
    89→
    90→For each user message or topic:
    91→
    92→**Relevance Analysis:**
    93→
    94→- Analyze the user's message/question for domain and expertise requirements
    95→- Identify which agents would naturally contribute based on their role, capabilities, and principles
    96→- Consider conversation context and previous agent contributions
    97→- Select 2-3 most relevant agents for balanced perspective
    98→
    99→**Priority Handling:**
   100→
   101→- If user addresses specific agent by name, prioritize that agent + 1-2 complementary agents
   102→- Rotate agent selection to ensure diverse participation over time
   103→- Enable natural cross-talk and agent-to-agent interactions
   104→
   105→### Conversation Orchestration
   106→
   107→Load step: `./steps/step-02-discussion-orchestration.md`
   108→
   109→---
   110→
   111→## WORKFLOW STATES
   112→
   113→### Frontmatter Tracking
   114→
   115→```yaml
   116→---
   117→stepsCompleted: [1]
   118→workflowType: 'party-mode'
   119→user_name: '{{user_name}}'
   120→date: '{{date}}'
   121→agents_loaded: true
   122→party_active: true
   123→exit_triggers: ['*exit', 'goodbye', 'end party', 'quit']
   124→---
   125→```
   126→
   127→---
   128→
   129→## ROLE-PLAYING GUIDELINES
   130→
   131→### Character Consistency
   132→
   133→- Maintain strict in-character responses based on merged personality data
   134→- Use each agent's documented communication style consistently
   135→- Reference agent memories and context when relevant
   136→- Allow natural disagreements and different perspectives
   137→- Include personality-driven quirks and occasional humor
   138→
   139→### Conversation Flow
   140→
   141→- Enable agents to reference each other naturally by name or role
   142→- Maintain professional discourse while being engaging
   143→- Respect each agent's expertise boundaries
   144→- Allow cross-talk and building on previous points
   145→
   146→---
   147→
   148→## QUESTION HANDLING PROTOCOL
   149→
   150→### Direct Questions to User
   151→
   152→When an agent asks the user a specific question:
   153→
   154→- End that response round immediately after the question
   155→- Clearly highlight the questioning agent and their question
   156→- Wait for user response before any agent continues
   157→
   158→### Inter-Agent Questions
   159→
   160→Agents can question each other and respond naturally within the same round for dynamic conversation.
   161→
   162→---
   163→
   164→## EXIT CONDITIONS
   165→
   166→### Automatic Triggers
   167→
   168→Exit party mode when user message contains any exit triggers:
   169→
   170→- `*exit`, `goodbye`, `end party`, `quit`
   171→
   172→### Graceful Conclusion
   173→
   174→If conversation naturally concludes:
   175→
   176→- Ask user if they'd like to continue or end party mode
   177→- Exit gracefully when user indicates completion
   178→
   179→---
   180→
   181→## MODERATION NOTES
   182→
   183→**Quality Control:**
   184→
   185→- If discussion becomes circular, have bmad-master summarize and redirect
   186→- Balance fun and productivity based on conversation tone
   187→- Ensure all agents stay true to their merged personalities
   188→- Exit gracefully when user indicates completion
   189→
   190→**Conversation Management:**
   191→
   192→- Rotate agent participation to ensure inclusive discussion
   193→- Handle topic drift while maintaining productive conversation
   194→- Facilitate cross-agent collaboration and knowledge sharing
   195→

## 6. Repository cloning or sparse checkout references

Found in `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/qmd-registry.md`:

**Explicitly REJECTED - Source Clone Indexing (lines 77-81):**
- "Clone the target repository and index all source files into QMD."
- **Rejection reason:** Freshness problem. A cloned repo is a snapshot pretending to be current. No clear owner for refresh lifecycle. The provenance map already records `file:line` for every export, and ast-grep provides structural queries against local source.

**Explicitly REJECTED - Source-Map Indexing (lines 83-87):**
- "Index the GitHub API tree response (directory structure + file metadata)"
- Rejection: No workflow consumer

**Approach used instead:**
- `gh_bridge.list_tree()` for remote repository structure mapping (non-destructive)
- Direct source file reading via `gh_bridge.read_file()` without full clones
- Local file system access when source is already checked out

     1→# Progressive Capability
     2→
     3→## Principle
     4→
     5→Every capability tier — Quick, Forge, and Deep — produces legitimate, useful skills. Tiers describe what tools are available, not a quality hierarchy. A Quick skill is complete at its tier; a Deep skill adds depth, not correctness. Framing is always positive: describe what each tier enables, never what it lacks.
     6→
     7→## Rationale
     8→
     9→Skill Forge operates in diverse environments. Some users have ast-grep, GitHub CLI, and QMD installed; others have none. The progressive capability model ensures Ferris adapts to the environment without making users feel their setup is insufficient.
    10→
    11→Without progressive capability:
    12→- Users without ast-grep see error messages or degraded output
    13→- Quick tier skills feel like failures rather than valid deliverables
    14→- Workflow steps contain conditional logic for missing tools scattered throughout
    15→
    16→With progressive capability:
    17→- Each tier has a clear identity and positive capability description
    18→- Workflow behavior adapts cleanly based on the detected tier
    19→- Users understand what their tier enables and what upgrading would add
    20→
    21→## Tier Definitions
    22→
    23→| Tier | Requirements | Capabilities | Identity |
    24→| --- | --- | --- | --- |
    25→| Quick | No tools required | Fast, template-driven, package-name resolution | Speed-first skill creation |
    26→| Forge | ast-grep available | AST-backed structural analysis, line-level citations | Precision-focused compilation |
    27→| Deep | ast-grep + gh + qmd | AST + GitHub exploration + QMD synthesis, maximum provenance | Full intelligence pipeline |
    28→
    29→## Pattern Examples
    30→
    31→### Example 1: Tool Detection and Tier Assignment
    32→
    33→**Context:** The setup-forge workflow detects available tools and assigns a tier.
    34→
    35→**Implementation:** Each tool is verified by executing its version command — presence in PATH is insufficient. The tier is the highest level where all required tools pass verification:
    36→
    37→```
    38→ast-grep --version  → required for Forge
    39→gh --version        → required for Deep
    40→qmd status          → required for Deep
    41→```
    42→
    43→If ast-grep passes but gh fails, the tier is Forge (not a "degraded Deep").
    44→
    45→**Key Points:**
    46→- Tools must be functional, not just installed
    47→- Tier assignment is deterministic — no ambiguity
    48→- The result is written to `forge-tier.yaml` in the sidecar and persists across sessions
    49→
    50→### Example 2: Positive Tier Framing
    51→
    52→**Context:** Reporting the detected tier to the user.
    53→
    54→**Implementation:**
    55→```
    56→Quick:  "Fast, template-driven skill creation — package-name resolution and
    57→         source reading. Skills ready in minutes."
    58→
    59→Forge:  "AST-backed structural analysis — line-level citations with verified
    60→         signatures. Precision-focused compilation."
    61→
    62→Deep:   "Full intelligence pipeline — AST verification plus GitHub exploration
    63→         and QMD knowledge synthesis. Maximum provenance depth."
    64→```
    65→
    66→**Key Points:**
    67→- Each description leads with what the tier does, not what it misses
    68→- No tier mentions another tier's capabilities as something it lacks
    69→- Quick is "fast" not "limited"; Forge is "precise" not "partial"
    70→
    71→### Example 3: Tier-Adaptive Extraction
    72→
    73→**Context:** The create-skill extraction step adapts its strategy based on the forge tier.
    74→
    75→**Implementation:**
    76→- **Quick:** Reads source files, applies pattern matching for exports and signatures, produces T1-low citations. Uses `gh_bridge` for file structure when available as a convenience, not a requirement.
    77→- **Forge:** Uses `ast_bridge.scan_definitions()` for structural parsing, produces T1 citations with exact AST node types. Falls back to source reading for files ast-grep cannot parse.
    78→- **Deep:** Identical extraction to Forge, plus QMD enrichment in a separate step. T2 annotations layer on top of the T1 base without replacing it.
    79→
    80→**Key Points:**
    81→- Extraction quality increases with tier, but every tier produces complete output
    82→- Forge and Deep share the same extraction core — Deep adds enrichment, not better parsing
    83→- Fallback within a tier (e.g., ast-grep fails on one file) degrades gracefully to T1-low for that file only
    84→
    85→### Example 4: Tier-Adaptive Scoring
    86→
    87→**Context:** The test-skill workflow adjusts scoring weights based on the forge tier.
    88→
    89→**Implementation:**
    90→- **Quick (Naive mode):** Export Coverage 50%, Signature Accuracy 30%, Type Coverage 20%. No coherence check — AST verification unavailable.
    91→- **Forge (Contextual mode):** Export Coverage 40%, Signature Accuracy 25%, Type Coverage 15%, Coherence 20%. Full AST-backed verification.
    92→- **Deep (Contextual mode):** Same weights as Forge, plus cross-repo verification and QMD coherence checks feed into the coherence score.
    93→
    94→**Key Points:**
    95→- Pass threshold (80%) is the same across all tiers — quality bar is tier-independent
    96→- Weight redistribution reflects what each tier can actually measure
    97→- A Quick skill scoring 85% and a Deep skill scoring 85% both pass — different measurements, same bar
    98→
    99→## Graceful Degradation
   100→
   101→When a tool becomes unavailable mid-session (e.g., ast-grep uninstalled between runs):
   102→1. Setup-forge re-detects and downgrades the tier
   103→2. Existing skills retain their original citations — they don't retroactively lose confidence
   104→3. New extractions use the current tier's capabilities
   105→4. The tier change is logged in the sidecar for audit trail
   106→
   107→## Anti-Patterns
   108→
   109→- Describing Quick tier as "basic" or "limited" — it is fast and legitimate
   110→- Requiring ast-grep for workflows that work fine without it (setup-forge, brief-skill)
   111→- Mixing tier capabilities in a single extraction (e.g., T1 citations when only source reading was used)
   112→- Failing a workflow because the tier is "too low" — adapt behavior instead
   113→
   114→## Related Fragments
   115→
   116→- [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md) — how tier determines available confidence levels
   117→- [skill-lifecycle.md](skill-lifecycle.md) — how tier affects workflow selection and artifact flow
   118→- [zero-hallucination.md](zero-hallucination.md) — Quick tier has lower confidence but the same integrity standard
   119→
   120→_Source: distilled from forge-tier.yaml, tier-rules.md, and setup-forge workflow_
   121→
```

🎉 **PARTY MODE — REVIEW PANEL ASSEMBLED** 🎉

Welcome Armel! I've loaded the full impact surface for issue #18. Here's our expert panel:

- 🏗️ **Morgan** (Module Creation Master) — systems impact
- 🔄 **Wendy** (Workflow Building Master) — step integrity
- 🏗️ **Winston** (Architect) — technical design

**The topic: Validating the edit plan for `step-03-extract.md` (issue #18) — avoiding breaking changes and missing impacts.**

Let me kick off the discussion with the research findings. Team, over to you.

---

### 🏗️ Morgan (Module Creation Master)

Hold on, team. Before anyone nods along with the proposed fix, I've found something that stops us in our tracks.

**The `qmd-registry.md` knowledge fragment explicitly rejected Source Clone Indexing.** Line 77-81:

> *"Clone the target repository and index all source files into QMD."*
> **Rejection reason:** *Freshness problem. A cloned repo is a snapshot pretending to be current. No clear owner for refresh lifecycle.*

Now — the issue proposes cloning into `forge-data/{skill-name}/source/`. That's the same pattern we already rejected. The freshness objection applies equally here: who owns the refresh? When does a stale clone get re-cloned? What happens when `update-skill` runs against a stale clone?

But here's the nuance: the rejection was about **QMD indexing** of cloned source, not about **ephemeral cloning for extraction**. If we treat the clone as a **disposable, session-scoped artifact** — clone, extract, discard — the freshness problem evaporates. The clone never pretends to be current because it doesn't persist.

**My recommendation:** If we clone, it must be ephemeral. The plan says `forge-data/{skill-name}/source/` — but `forge-data/` is a persistent directory. That's the wrong home. We need a temp path, or we need to document the lifecycle clearly.

---

### 🔄 Wendy (Workflow Building Master)

Morgan raises the architectural concern. Let me trace the **workflow impact** — because the plan only touches `create-skill/step-03-extract.md`, but the same problem exists in **three other workflows**:

| Workflow | Step | Uses AST? | Same Issue? |
|----------|------|-----------|-------------|
| `create-skill` | `step-03-extract` | Yes (Forge/Deep) | **YES — this is the target** |
| `update-skill` | `step-03-re-extract` | Yes (Forge/Deep) | **YES — same silent degradation** |
| `create-stack-skill` | `step-04-parallel-extract` | Yes (Forge/Deep) | **YES — parallel extraction** |
| `quick-skill` | `step-03-quick-extract` | No (Quick only) | No — by design |

**If we fix only `create-skill`, the other two workflows still silently degrade.** That's inconsistent behavior — a user running `update-skill` at Forge tier against the same remote repo would get T1-low without warning while `create-skill` properly resolves the source.

**Section renumbering risk:** The plan renumbers Sections 3-6 to 4-7. No external files reference these section numbers by number — the step file is self-contained. Gate 2 is referenced by name ("Gate 2"), not section number. So renumbering is **safe**.

**State flow concern:** Step-01 (`step-01-load-brief.md`, line 107-114) resolves the source location and stores `owner, repo, branch, file tree` for remote repos OR `local path, file listing` for local repos. The new clone section in step-03 would need to consume that state and **update the working path** so downstream AST calls use the local clone. That state mutation needs to be explicit.

---

### 🏗️ Winston (Architect)

The pragmatic question is: **what does ast-grep actually need?**

`ast-grep` operates on local files. Period. There's no remote mode. So when `source_repo` is a GitHub URL and the tier is Forge or Deep, we have exactly two options:

1. **Clone the repo** — gives ast-grep local files to work with
2. **Don't clone, degrade explicitly** — stay on source reading, warn the user

The issue frames option 1 as the obvious fix. But let me push back on the assumption that AST extraction for remote repos is the **default** behavior.

**Consider the `progressive-capability.md` philosophy** (line 5):

> *"Every capability tier produces legitimate, useful skills. Tiers describe what tools are available, not a quality hierarchy."*

And line 83:

> *"Fallback within a tier (e.g., ast-grep fails on one file) degrades gracefully to T1-low for that file only"*

The knowledge base is telling us: **graceful degradation is the designed behavior**, not a bug. What IS a bug is doing it **silently**. The issue correctly identifies the silence as the core problem.

**My counter-proposal for your consideration:**

Rather than adding a clone step (which introduces git as a new dependency, network operations in the extraction pipeline, and lifecycle questions Morgan raised), consider:

**Option A — Clone (full fix):**
- Add clone step, get real AST extraction for remote repos
- Pro: Issue #18 fully resolved
- Con: New dependency (git), network in extraction, lifecycle management, impacts 3 workflows

**Option B — Explicit degradation with guidance (minimal fix):**
- Replace silent degradation with explicit warning
- Tell the user: *"Remote source detected at Forge/Deep tier. AST extraction requires local files. Clone the repo locally and update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path for T1 confidence."*
- Pro: Zero new dependencies, zero lifecycle issues, user retains control
- Con: Extra manual step for users with remote repos

**Option C — Hybrid (balanced fix):**
- Offer to clone ephemerally at extraction time — shallow clone, extract, then **clean up**
- If clone fails (network, permissions), degrade explicitly with Option B's guidance
- Pro: Best of both worlds
- Con: Most complex to implement

---

### 🏗️ Morgan (Module Creation Master)

Winston's breakdown is excellent. Let me add one more dimension: **the `update-skill` workflow**.

`update-skill/step-03-re-extract.md` operates on **changed files only** from a manifest. If we clone in `create-skill`, we'd need to clone again in `update-skill` — but sparse-checkout scoped to only the changed files. That's a different clone strategy entirely.

Also, `step-01-load-brief.md` (line 108) already calls `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` — it **verifies** the remote repo exists. But it never persists the tree locally. If we go with Option A or C, the clone should happen in **step-01** (source resolution) not step-03 (extraction). That way:

1. Source resolution is complete by the time extraction starts
2. All downstream steps (step-02 ecosystem check, step-03 extract) can assume local files
3. The state contract is cleaner: "source is always local by the time extraction runs"

---

### 🔄 Wendy (Workflow Building Master)

Morgan, that's a good point about placement, but it **changes the scope**. Moving the clone to step-01 means modifying **two files** per workflow instead of one, and it changes the step-01 contract which is currently "load and validate, don't perform operations."

Let me summarize the **breaking change risk** for each option:

| Risk | Option A (Clone) | Option B (Warn) | Option C (Hybrid) |
|------|------------------|-----------------|-------------------|
| New file changes | 1 file (step-03) or 2 files (step-01 + step-03) per workflow | 1 file (step-03) per workflow | 1-2 files per workflow |
| Workflows impacted | 3 (create, update, stack) | 3 (create, update, stack) | 3 (create, update, stack) |
| New dependencies | git (clone) | None | git (optional) |
| State contract change | Yes (source path mutation) | No | Conditional |
| `extraction-patterns.md` update | Yes (new remote resolution strategy) | Yes (warning text) | Yes (both) |
| Knowledge fragment update | `qmd-registry.md` needs carve-out for ephemeral clones | None | `qmd-registry.md` carve-out |
| Failure modes | Network failure mid-extraction | None new | Network failure with fallback |

**The minimum safe edit** that addresses issue #18 without introducing breaking changes is **Option B**: replace silent degradation with explicit warning in all three extraction steps, plus update `extraction-patterns.md`. Zero new dependencies, zero state contract changes, zero lifecycle questions.

Option A or C can be a **follow-up issue** once the design is validated.

---

### 🏗️ Winston (Architect)

Agreed on the staged approach. One final technical note: the current line 91 says:

> *"Degrade to Quick tier extraction silently. Note the degradation in context for the evidence report."*

The fix should:
1. **Remove "silently"**
2. **Add explicit user-facing warning** with actionable guidance
3. **Preserve the evidence report notation** (that part is correct)
4. Apply consistently to `update-skill/step-03-re-extract` and `create-stack-skill/step-04-parallel-extract`

---

**Armel, the team has surfaced these key findings:**

1. **The clone approach conflicts with a rejected candidate in `qmd-registry.md`** — needs careful scoping if pursued
2. **Three workflows are affected, not just one** — `create-skill`, `update-skill`, `create-stack-skill`
3. **The minimum safe fix is Option B** (explicit warning, no clone) — addressing the *silence* which is the actual bug
4. **Clone-based resolution (Option A/C) should be a separate follow-up** with its own design

**What direction would you like to take?**

> DEVELOPER

Based on the thorough analysis from the review panel, **Option B (Explicit degradation with guidance)** is absolutely the right direction to take for now. 

It perfectly aligns with the progressive capability philosophy: graceful degradation is a feature, but silent degradation is a bug. By choosing Option B, we fix the bug (the silence) without introducing architectural regressions, lifecycle management overhead, or new dependencies like `git`.

Here is the revised action plan to implement Option B across the affected workflows:

### Revised Edit Plan: Option B (Explicit Warning)

**1. Target `create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`**
*   **Remove:** The word "silently" from the degradation instruction.
*   **Add:** An explicit user-facing warning instruction when remote source is detected at Forge/Deep tier.
*   *Warning Text Guidance:* "⚠ Warn the user explicitly: AST extraction requires local files. Remote source detected. Degrading to Quick tier extraction (source reading) for this run. For T1 confidence, clone the repository locally and update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path."

**2. Target `update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`**
*   Apply the exact same explicit warning logic. Users updating a skill against a remote repo should receive the same actionable guidance instead of a silent fallback.

**3. Target `create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-04-parallel-extract.md`**
*   Apply the exact same explicit warning logic for parallel extractions against remote sources.

**4. Target `create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`**
*   Update the fallback rules to explicitly mandate this warning behavior when dropping from Forge/Deep tier to Quick tier due to remote URLs.

By doing this, we maintain the integrity of the `qmd-registry.md` rules (no messy source clones), keep the state flow clean, and empower the user with actionable feedback. We can always explore the ephemeral cloning (Option C) as a future enhancement once the core workflows are stabilized.

Does this revised plan look good to proceed with?

    63→**Forge Tier (adds to Quick):**
    64→- Use ast_bridge to analyze structural exports from library source
    65→- Extract function signatures, type definitions, class hierarchies
    66→- Map parameter types and return types
    67→- Confidence: T1 (AST-verified structural extraction)
    68→
    69→**Deep Tier (adds to Forge):**
    70→- Use qmd_bridge for temporal usage evolution
    71→- Identify deprecated patterns and migration paths
    72→- Track API changes across project history
    73→- Confidence: T2 (QMD-enriched temporal context)
    74→
    75→### 2. Launch Parallel Extraction
    76→
    77→**Launch subprocesses in parallel** (max_parallel_generation) — one per confirmed library:
    78→
    79→Each subprocess:
    80→1. Reads all files importing the library (from step 03 file lists)
    81→2. Extracts key exports used in this project (functions, classes, types, constants)
    82→3. Identifies usage patterns (initialization, configuration, common call patterns)
    83→4. Labels confidence tier based on extraction method
    84→5. Returns structured extraction to parent:
    85→
    86→```
    87→{
    88→  library: "name",
    89→  version: "from_manifest",
    90→  exports_found: ["fn1", "fn2", "Type1"],
    91→  usage_patterns: ["pattern description with file:line"],
    92→  confidence: "T1|T1-low|T2",
    93→  files_analyzed: count,
    94→  warnings: []
    95→}
    96→```
    97→
    98→**If parallel subprocess unavailable:** Process libraries sequentially in main thread. Report progress after each library.
    99→
   100→### 3. Handle Extraction Failures
   101→
   102→For each library extraction:
   103→
   104→**Success:** Store extraction result.
   105→
   106→**Partial failure:** Store partial result with warnings, continue with other libraries.
   107→
   108→**Complete failure:** Log failure reason, exclude from stack skill, note in report.
   109→
   110→"**Warning:** Extraction failed for {library}: {reason}. Excluding from stack skill."
   111→
   112→**If ALL extractions fail:** HALT — cannot produce meaningful stack skill.
   113→
   114→### 4. Display Extraction Summary
   115→
   116→"**Library extraction complete.**
   117→
   118→| Library | Exports | Patterns | Confidence | Status |
   119→|---------|---------|----------|------------|--------|
   120→| {name} | {count} | {count} | {tier} | ✓ |
   121→| {name} | {count} | {count} | {tier} | ✓ |
   122→| {name} | — | — | — | ⚠ partial |
   123→
   124→**Results:** {success_count}/{total_count} libraries extracted
   125→**Confidence distribution:** T1: {count}, T1-low: {count}, T2: {count}
   126→{If warnings:} **Warnings:** {warning_count} issues noted
   127→
   128→**Proceeding to integration detection...**"
   129→
   130→### 5. Auto-Proceed to Next Step
   131→
   132→Load, read the full file and then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
   133→
   134→---
   135→
   136→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   137→
   138→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   139→
   140→- All confirmed libraries have extraction attempted
   141→- Extractions cite actual file:line references
   142→- Confidence tiers correctly assigned per extraction method
   143→- Partial failures handled gracefully (skip library, continue)
   144→- Extraction summary displayed with accurate counts
   145→- Auto-proceeded to step 05
   146→
   147→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   148→
   149→- Fabricating exports not found in source code
   150→- Not assigning confidence tiers to extractions
   151→- Halting on single library failure (should degrade gracefully)
   152→- Analyzing cross-library integrations (step 05's job)
   153→- Returning raw file contents instead of structured extractions
   154→
   155→**Master Rule:** Extract per-library only. Every export must trace to actual code. Degrade gracefully on individual failures.
   156→
```

Now I have all the information needed to compile a complete and precise review. Here is my report.

---

## Review: GitHub Issue #18 Fix — Explicit AST Extraction Warnings

Reviewing 5 files across create-skill, update-skill, create-stack-skill, and the knowledge base for consistency, correctness, and integration quality.

---

## Critical Issues

### Issue 1 — Wrong variable name in update-skill/step-03-re-extract.md (source_repo vs source_root)

**Confidence: High (95)**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`, lines 62, 66, 70

The update-skill workflow does not use `source_repo`. The update-skill context chain is unambiguous: step-01-init loads `source_root` from `metadata.json` (line 78 and 134 of step-01-init.md), step-02-detect-changes passes it forward as `source_root` (line 50 and 61 of step-02-detect-changes.md), and the source is always resolved to a path before step-03 runs (step-01 halts or prompts the user if the source path is invalid — it never passes a raw remote URL forward).

The check in re-extract step-03 reads:

```
If `source_repo` is a remote URL ... update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path.
```

This is wrong on two fronts:
1. The variable in context at this point is `source_root`, not `source_repo`. There is no `source_repo` in the update-skill context — there is no brief; the source location comes from `provenance-map.json` via metadata.json.
2. The warning instructs the user to update "`source_repo` in your brief," but update-skill has no brief. The correct instruction would be to re-run create-skill with a local path and then re-run update-skill, or to manually correct the `source_root` stored in provenance metadata.

This means the check will either never trigger (the variable name never matches) or confuse the user with an actionable instruction that references a non-existent file in the update-skill workflow. The bug both undermines the fix's purpose and produces misleading remediation guidance.

**Fix:** Replace `source_repo` with `source_root` in lines 62, 66, and 70. Update the actionable guidance to reference the actual remediation path for update-skill, which has no brief. For example:

```
If `source_root` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:

⚠️ Warn the user explicitly:

"Remote source detected at {tier} tier. AST extraction requires local files — degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and re-run [CS] Create Skill with the local path, then update this skill."
```

---

### Issue 2 — create-stack-skill/step-04 remote check uses undefined "library source" field

**Confidence: High (85)**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-04-parallel-extract.md`, lines 57–65

The remote source check reads:

```
For each library in confirmed_dependencies, if the library source is a remote URL ...
```

The `confirmed_dependencies[]` array is built in step-03-rank-and-confirm. Inspecting that step, entries contain: `library_name`, `import_count`, `files`, and `category`. There is no `library source` field — dependencies are detected from manifest files (package.json, Cargo.toml, etc.) and ranked by import frequency. They are package names, not source paths.

The check is therefore unevaluable as written: "the library source" refers to a field that does not exist in `confirmed_dependencies`. An LLM executing this step cannot determine whether any library is "remote" or "local" because no source path is tracked for individual libraries in the create-stack-skill workflow — the workflow analyzes libraries as used within the current project's source tree, not by fetching the library's own source repository.

This means the remote degradation check will either always silently pass (no field to test, so the condition is never true) or be hallucinated into something unpredictable, which is exactly the silent-degradation behavior the issue was meant to fix.

**Fix:** Clarify what "library source" means in this workflow context. If create-stack-skill only reads files within the current project (which import the library) and never fetches the library's own remote repo, the remote-source check is inapplicable and should be removed or replaced with a note explaining it doesn't apply. If the intent is to support pointing at a library's source repo, the data model for `confirmed_dependencies` needs a `source_path` field defined in step-03.

---

## Important Issues

### Issue 3 — Warning text in create-stack-skill diverges from the other two steps on the user-facing actionable instruction

**Confidence: High (88)**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-04-parallel-extract.md`, line 61

The warning in step-04-parallel-extract reads:

```
"...update the source reference to a local path."
```

The warnings in create-skill/step-03 and update-skill/step-03 both read:

```
"...update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path."
```

The create-stack-skill version omits the specific field name and document name. While the vagueness partially stems from the undefined "library source" problem in Issue 2, even in the hypothetical where the field were defined, the instruction should be specific and consistent. "Update the source reference" tells the user nothing actionable: which field, in which file?

This also breaks consistency criterion 1 from the review scope. The three steps should share identical pattern and language for the same class of degradation.

**Fix:** Once the field name is clarified (Issue 2), use the same phrasing template as the other two steps, specifying the field name and the file to edit.

---

### Issue 4 — extraction-patterns.md "Per-File AST Failure" section is missing from the SUCCESS/FAILURE metrics in create-stack-skill/step-04

**Confidence: Medium (80)**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-04-parallel-extract.md`, SUCCESS/FAILURE metrics section (lines 154–173)

`extraction-patterns.md` defines three degradation rules: remote source, AST tool unavailable, and per-file AST failure. The per-file AST failure rule (fall back to T1-low for the individual file, continue with others) is codified in create-skill/step-03 line 107–110 and in extraction-patterns.md lines 92–100, but create-stack-skill/step-04 has no equivalent "handle individual file parse failure" rule — only a "partial failure" and "complete failure" at the library level (lines 122–130).

More critically, the SUCCESS metrics for step-04 (lines 158–164) do not include "partial library failures handled gracefully (T1-low for failing files, T1 for others)." Without this, an LLM executing the step has no success criterion that validates file-level graceful degradation within a single library extraction, only library-level graceful degradation.

**Fix:** Add a per-file failure handling rule to step-04's extraction logic (parallel with create-skill/step-03 lines 107–110) and add a corresponding SUCCESS criterion. Also add it to extraction-patterns.md's codification section as applying to all three workflows, not just create-skill.

---

### Issue 5 — progressive-capability.md does not mention the explicit warning requirement for remote sources

**Confidence: High (82)**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/progressive-capability.md`

The "Graceful Degradation" section (lines 99–105) and "Anti-Patterns" section (lines 107–112) were not updated to reflect the issue #18 fix. Specifically:

- The Graceful Degradation section describes tool unavailability mid-session but makes no mention of remote source as a degradation trigger.
- The Anti-Patterns section does not list "silent degradation when source_repo is remote at Forge/Deep tier" as a forbidden pattern, even though extraction-patterns.md now explicitly forbids it (line 80: "Silent degradation is forbidden").
- Example 3 (Tier-Adaptive Extraction, lines 71–83) describes fallback behavior without mentioning the remote-source case at all, leaving the knowledge fragment incomplete relative to the new rules.

This creates a consistency gap: extraction-patterns.md now codifies the rule authoritatively, but progressive-capability.md — which is the primary knowledge fragment for understanding tier behavior — does not reflect it. An LLM consulting only progressive-capability.md would have no basis for understanding when to warn vs. silently degrade.

**Fix:** Add a bullet to the Graceful Degradation section covering the remote source trigger. Add "silent degradation when source is remote at Forge/Deep tier" to the Anti-Patterns list. Optionally update Example 3 to reference the remote-source warning path.

---

## No Issues Found

**Criterion 3 (Logic placement):** In create-skill/step-03 and update-skill/step-03, the remote source check is correctly placed at the start of the execution section, before any extraction strategy is applied. The check gates the entire tier-dependent strategy, which is the correct location in the mandatory sequence.

**Criterion 4 (Formatting):** All three step files use the same markdown structure for the warning block: bold "Warn the user explicitly:" label, blockquoted warning text, then a prose instruction. This is consistent with surrounding content style in each file.

**Criterion 6 (Breaking changes to Quick tier flow):** The Quick tier flow is not affected. The remote source check is gated with "AND tier is Forge or Deep," so Quick tier executes the Quick extraction strategy regardless. The Gate 2 summary in create-skill/step-03 (lines 135–162) is structurally untouched; the `{warnings if any files skipped or degraded}` placeholder already accounts for degradation warnings in the summary output.

---

    95→- Pass threshold (80%) is the same across all tiers — quality bar is tier-independent
    96→- Weight redistribution reflects what each tier can actually measure
    97→- A Quick skill scoring 85% and a Deep skill scoring 85% both pass — different measurements, same bar
    98→
    99→## Graceful Degradation
   100→
   101→When a tool becomes unavailable mid-session (e.g., ast-grep uninstalled between runs):
   102→1. Setup-forge re-detects and downgrades the tier
   103→2. Existing skills retain their original citations — they don't retroactively lose confidence
   104→3. New extractions use the current tier's capabilities
   105→4. The tier change is logged in the sidecar for audit trail
   106→
   107→## Anti-Patterns
   108→
   109→- Describing Quick tier as "basic" or "limited" — it is fast and legitimate
   110→- Requiring ast-grep for workflows that work fine without it (setup-forge, brief-skill)
   111→- Mixing tier capabilities in a single extraction (e.g., T1 citations when only source reading was used)
   112→- Failing a workflow because the tier is "too low" — adapt behavior instead

 3. New extractions use the current tier's capabilities
 4. The tier change is logged in the sidecar for audit trail
 
+When source is remote (GitHub URL) and tier is Forge or Deep:
+1. AST extraction requires local files — ast-grep cannot operate on remote URLs
+2. The extraction step warns the user explicitly before degrading to source reading (T1-low)
+3. The warning includes actionable guidance: clone locally and update `source_repo` to a local path
+4. Silent degradation is forbidden — the user must always know when AST extraction was skipped and why
+
 ## Anti-Patterns
 
 - Describing Quick tier as "basic" or "limited" — it is fast and legitimate
 - Requiring ast-grep for workflows that work fine without it (setup-forge, brief-skill)
 - Mixing tier capabilities in a single extraction (e.g., T1 citations when only source reading was used)
 - Failing a workflow because the tier is "too low" — adapt behavior instead
+- Silent degradation when source is remote at Forge/Deep tier — always warn with actionable guidance
 
 ## Related Fragments
 
diff --git a/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md b/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md
index 26faf18..4d069b4 100644
--- a/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md
+++ b/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md
@@ -61,3 +61,39 @@ Same extraction as Forge tier. Deep tier adds enrichment in step-04, not extract
 ### Confidence
 - Extraction: same as Forge (T1)
 - Enrichment annotations added in step-04: T2
+
+---
+
+## Tier Degradation Rules
+
+### Remote Source at Forge/Deep Tier
+
+When `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) and the tier is Forge or Deep:
+
+- **ast-grep requires local files** — it cannot operate on remote URLs
+- The extraction step MUST warn the user explicitly before degrading
+- Warning must include actionable guidance: clone locally and update `source_repo`
+- Extraction proceeds using Quick tier strategy (source reading via gh_bridge)
+- All results labeled T1-low with `[SRC:...]` citations
+- The degradation reason is recorded in the evidence report
+
+Silent degradation is **forbidden**. The user must always know when AST extraction was skipped and why.
+
+### AST Tool Unavailable at Forge/Deep Tier
+
+When the tier is Forge or Deep but ast-grep is not functional:
+
+- The extraction step MUST warn the user explicitly before degrading
+- Warning must include actionable guidance: run [SF] Setup Forge to detect tools
+- Extraction proceeds using Quick tier strategy
+- All results labeled T1-low
+- The degradation reason is recorded in the evidence report
+
+### Per-File AST Failure
+
+When ast-grep fails on an individual file (parse error, unsupported syntax):
+
+- Fall back to source reading for **that file only**
+- Other files continue with AST extraction
+- The affected file's results are labeled T1-low; unaffected files retain T1
+- Log a warning noting which file degraded and why

+
+Proceed with Quick tier extraction strategy below. Note the tier degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
+
+If `source_repo` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
+
 **Quick Tier (No AST tools):**
 
 1. Use `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` to map source structure (if remote)

    69→### 3. Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction
    70→
    71→**Remote Source Resolution Check (Forge/Deep only):**
    72→
    73→If `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
    74→
    75→⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:**
    76→
    77→"Remote source detected at {tier} tier. AST extraction requires local files — degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path."
    78→
    79→Proceed with Quick tier extraction strategy below. Note the tier degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
    80→
    81→If `source_repo` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
    82→
    83→**Quick Tier (No AST tools):**
    84→
    85→1. Use `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` to map source structure (if remote)
    86→2. Identify entry points: index files, main exports, public modules
    87→3. Use `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, path)` to read each entry point
    88→4. Extract from source text: exported function names, parameter lists, return types
    89→5. Infer types from JSDoc, docstrings, type annotations
    90→6. Confidence: All results T1-low — `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]`
    91→
    92→**Forge/Deep Tier (AST available):**
    93→
    94→1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
    95→2. Use `ast_bridge.scan_definitions(path, language)` for each file in scope
    96→3. For each export: extract function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
    97→4. Use `ast_bridge.detect_co_imports(path, libraries[])` to find integration points
    98→5. Build extraction rules YAML data for reproducibility
    99→6. Confidence: All results T1 — `[AST:{file}:L{line}]`
   100→
   101→**If AST tool is unavailable at Forge/Deep tier:**
   102→
   103→⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:** "AST tools are unavailable — extraction will use source reading (T1-low). Run [SF] Setup Forge to detect and configure AST tools for T1 confidence."
   104→
   105→Degrade to Quick tier extraction. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
   106→
   107→**For each file — handle failures gracefully:**
   108→
   109→- If a file cannot be read: log warning, skip file, continue with remaining files
   110→- If AST parsing fails on a file: fall back to source reading for that file, continue
   111→
   112→### 4. Build Extraction Inventory

    75→## Rejected Candidates
    76→
    77→### Source Clone Indexing
    78→
    79→**Proposal:** Clone the target repository and index all source files into QMD.
    80→
    81→**Rejection reason:** Freshness problem. A cloned repo is a snapshot pretending to be current. No clear owner for refresh lifecycle. The provenance map already records `file:line` for every export, and ast-grep provides structural queries against local source. QMD indexing of raw source adds noise, not signal.
    82→
    83→### Source-Map Indexing
    84→
    85→**Proposal:** Index the GitHub API tree response (directory structure + file metadata) as a lightweight `{name}-source-map` collection.
    86→
    87→**Rejection reason:** No workflow consumer. Audit-skill already has file paths from provenance-map.json. The source-map would only serve ad-hoc developer queries, which don't justify the collection overhead.
    88→
    89→### Analysis Report Indexing

```
    65→---
    66→
    67→## Tier Degradation Rules
    68→
    69→### Remote Source at Forge/Deep Tier
    70→
    71→When `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) and the tier is Forge or Deep:
    72→
    73→- **ast-grep requires local files** — it cannot operate on remote URLs
    74→- The extraction step MUST warn the user explicitly before degrading
    75→- Warning must include actionable guidance: clone locally and update `source_repo`
    76→- Extraction proceeds using Quick tier strategy (source reading via gh_bridge)
    77→- All results labeled T1-low with `[SRC:...]` citations
    78→- The degradation reason is recorded in the evidence report
    79→
    80→Silent degradation is **forbidden**. The user must always know when AST extraction was skipped and why.
    81→
    82→### AST Tool Unavailable at Forge/Deep Tier
    83→
    84→When the tier is Forge or Deep but ast-grep is not functional:
    85→
    86→- The extraction step MUST warn the user explicitly before degrading
    87→- Warning must include actionable guidance: run [SF] Setup Forge to detect tools
    88→- Extraction proceeds using Quick tier strategy
    89→- All results labeled T1-low
    90→- The degradation reason is recorded in the evidence report
    91→
    92→### Per-File AST Failure
    93→
    94→When ast-grep fails on an individual file (parse error, unsupported syntax):
    95→
    96→- Fall back to source reading for **that file only**
    97→- Other files continue with AST extraction
    98→- The affected file's results are labeled T1-low; unaffected files retain T1
    99→- Log a warning noting which file degraded and why
   100→
```

> AGENT

**Here's the current content relevant to Option C:**

The Option B warnings we just shipped are in place across all three extraction steps. The key section in `create-skill/step-03-extract.md` (lines 71-81) currently warns and degrades immediately:

```
If source_repo is a remote URL AND tier is Forge or Deep:
  ⚠️ Warn → degrade to Quick tier
```

The enhancement is to **attempt an ephemeral clone before degrading** — clone, extract with AST, clean up. Only fall back to the Option B warning if the clone fails.

**Design context from the party mode discussion:**

| Concern | Resolution |
|---------|------------|
| `qmd-registry.md` rejected cloning | That rejection was for **persistent QMD indexing** of cloned source. Ephemeral clone-extract-discard avoids the freshness problem entirely |
| Clone placement (step-01 vs step-03) | **Step-03** — keeps step-01's "load and validate only" contract intact. Clone is extraction's concern |
| Clone location | Ephemeral temp path, cleaned up after extraction completes |
| `create-stack-skill` | N/A — operates on local project files, no remote source to clone |
| Failure handling | Clone fails → fall back to Option B warning (already in place) |

**What would you like to change?**

Loading the edit type selection.

🎉 **PARTY MODE ACTIVATED!** 🎉

Welcome back Armel! Same review panel, new question — validating the Option C edit plan before we commit to it.

- 🏗️ **Morgan** (Module Creation Master) — architecture coherence
- 🔄 **Wendy** (Workflow Building Master) — step integrity
- 🏗️ **Winston** (Architect) — technical design

**The question: Is the 4-file edit plan for ephemeral cloning correct?**

---

### 🏗️ Morgan (Module Creation Master)

Let me challenge one item on this plan immediately: **file #4 — `qmd-registry.md`**.

The rejected candidate in that file is titled "Source Clone Indexing" — it's about cloning a repo **and indexing its files into QMD**. Our ephemeral clone has nothing to do with QMD. It's a local file resolution strategy for ast-grep. These are completely different operations in completely different systems.

Adding a "carve-out" to `qmd-registry.md` would be **mixing concerns**. That knowledge fragment is about QMD collection architecture — which documents get indexed, which don't, and why. An ephemeral clone for AST extraction is an extraction pipeline concern, not a QMD concern. It belongs in `extraction-patterns.md`, which is already in the plan as file #3.

**My recommendation: Drop file #4 from the plan.** The `qmd-registry.md` rejection stands as-is. It's about persistent QMD indexing. Our ephemeral clone doesn't contradict it because it doesn't touch QMD at all. No carve-out needed.

---

### 🔄 Wendy (Workflow Building Master)

Morgan's right — the scoping is wrong on file #4. Let me trace the flow for the remaining three files.

**File #1 — `create-skill/step-03-extract.md`:**

The current "Remote Source Resolution Check" warns and degrades. The replacement would:
1. Detect remote URL + Forge/Deep
2. Attempt ephemeral shallow clone
3. If success → proceed with Forge/Deep extraction on local clone → cleanup
4. If fail → fall back to Option B warning + Quick tier

This is **correctly placed** in step-03. The clone is extraction's concern, not init's. Step-01 already resolved `owner, repo, branch` — step-03 has everything it needs.

But I have a **sequencing concern**: cleanup. Where exactly does cleanup happen? After Section 3 (extraction)? After Section 4 (build inventory)? After Gate 2 (user confirms)? The clone needs to persist through the entire extraction pipeline but be cleaned up before the step completes. That means cleanup should happen **after Section 4 (Build Extraction Inventory) but before Section 5 (Gate 2 summary)** — by that point all data is in context and local files are no longer needed.

**File #2 — `update-skill/step-03-re-extract.md`:**

Same pattern, but note: update-skill extracts only **changed files** from a manifest. If we clone the whole repo but only need 3 files, that's wasteful. A **sparse-checkout** scoped to the changed files list would be more efficient. The edit should account for this: use the change manifest from step-02 to build a sparse-checkout filter.

**File #3 — `extraction-patterns.md`:**

This is the right place to codify the ephemeral clone strategy. It should sit in the "Remote Source at Forge/Deep Tier" section, between the current "ast-grep requires local files" statement and the "warn the user" fallback.

---

### 🏗️ Winston (Architect)

Good call from both Morgan and Wendy. Let me address the technical design questions.

**Dependency: `git`**

The plan adds `git` as an implicit dependency for Forge/Deep tier when source is remote. But consider: `gh` (GitHub CLI) is already a Deep tier requirement, and `gh` itself requires `git`. For Forge tier, `ast-grep` is the only requirement — `git` may not be present. So:

- **Deep tier:** `git` is effectively already available (via `gh` dependency) — safe to assume
- **Forge tier:** `git` is NOT guaranteed — we need a fallback

This means the ephemeral clone should check for `git` availability before attempting. If `git` is missing at Forge tier, fall back to Option B. This is a natural fit because the tool availability check already happens in setup-forge.

**Clone strategy:**

```
git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch {url} {temp_path}
```

Shallow clone (`--depth 1`) minimizes network and disk. `--single-branch` avoids fetching other branches. For update-skill with sparse-checkout (Wendy's point):

```
git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {url} {temp_path}
git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {changed_files...}
```

**Clone location:**

I'd recommend a **system temp path** rather than `forge-data/`. Something like:

```
{system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
```

This gives us:
- OS-managed cleanup if we crash
- No persistence questions in `forge-data/`
- Clear naming for debugging if cleanup fails
- No conflict with the `qmd-registry.md` rejection (not even in the same directory tree)

**Security note:** The clone URL comes from the user's `skill-brief.yaml` — user-controlled input. No injection risk since `git clone` treats the URL as a single argument, not a shell command. But we should validate the URL format before passing it to git.

---

### 🏗️ Morgan (Module Creation Master)

Winston raises a critical point about Forge vs Deep tier and `git` availability. Let me refine the plan scope:

**Revised edit plan — 3 files, not 4:**

| # | File | Change |
|---|------|--------|
| 1 | `create-skill/step-03-extract.md` | Replace remote source check: attempt ephemeral clone (if `git` available) → AST extract → cleanup after inventory built. Fall back to Option B warning if clone or `git` unavailable |
| 2 | `update-skill/step-03-re-extract.md` | Same pattern with sparse-checkout scoped to change manifest. Uses `source_root` |
| 3 | `extraction-patterns.md` | Update "Remote Source at Forge/Deep Tier" with ephemeral clone strategy, `git` availability gate, and cleanup lifecycle |

**Dropped:** `qmd-registry.md` — no carve-out needed, different concern entirely.

---

### 🔄 Wendy (Workflow Building Master)

One more thing — should `setup-forge` detect `git` availability? Currently `forge-tier.yaml` tracks `ast-grep`, `gh`, and `qmd`. If we add `git` as a conditional tool for ephemeral cloning, it would be cleaner to detect it in setup-forge and record it in the tier config, rather than having step-03 shell out to `git --version` at runtime.

But that's **scope creep** for this edit. Step-03 can check for `git` inline — it's a single `which git` or `git --version` check. We can formalize it in setup-forge as a follow-up.

**My verdict: The revised 3-file plan is correct. Proceed.**

---

**Team consensus, Armel:**

1. **Drop file #4** (`qmd-registry.md`) — ephemeral clone is not a QMD concern
2. **3-file plan is correct** — step-03 for both workflows + extraction-patterns.md
3. **Add `git` availability check** before attempting clone (not guaranteed at Forge tier)
4. **Use system temp path** — not `forge-data/`
5. **Cleanup after inventory built** (Section 4), before Gate 2 summary
6. **Sparse-checkout for update-skill** — scope to changed files only

**Would you like to proceed with the revised 3-file plan, or discuss further?**

```
     1→# Extraction Patterns by Tier
     2→
     3→## Quick Tier (No AST)
     4→
     5→Source reading via gh_bridge — infer exports from file structure and content.
     6→
     7→### Strategy
     8→1. `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` — map source structure
     9→2. Identify entry points: index files, main exports, public modules
    10→3. `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, path)` — read each entry point
    11→4. Extract: exported function names, parameter lists, return types (from signatures)
    12→5. Infer types from JSDoc, docstrings, type annotations in source
    13→
    14→### Confidence
    15→- All results: T1-low (source reading without structural verification)
    16→- No co-import detection available
    17→- No AST-backed line numbers
    18→
    19→### Supported Patterns
    20→- `export function name(...)` / `export const name = ...` (JS/TS)
    21→- `pub fn name(...)` (Rust)
    22→- `def name(...)` with `__all__` (Python)
    23→- `func Name(...)` (Go, capitalized = exported)
    24→
    25→---
    26→
    27→## Forge Tier (AST Available)
    28→
    29→Structural extraction via ast_bridge — verified exports with line-level citations.
    30→
    31→### Strategy
    32→1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
    33→2. `ast_bridge.scan_definitions(path, language)` — extract all exports
    34→3. For each export: function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
    35→4. `ast_bridge.detect_co_imports(path, libraries[])` — find integration points
    36→5. Build extraction rules YAML for reproducibility
    37→
    38→### Confidence
    39→- Exported functions with full signatures: T1 (AST-verified)
    40→- Type definitions and interfaces: T1
    41→- Co-import patterns: T1
    42→- Internal/private functions: excluded (not part of public API)
    43→
    44→### ast-grep Patterns
    45→- JS/TS: `export function $NAME($$$PARAMS): $RET` / `export const $NAME`
    46→- Rust: `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET`
    47→- Python: function definitions within `__all__` list
    48→- Go: capitalized function definitions
    49→
    50→---
    51→
    52→## Deep Tier (AST + QMD)
    53→
    54→Same extraction as Forge tier. Deep tier adds enrichment in step-04, not extraction.
    55→
    56→### Strategy
    57→- Identical to Forge tier extraction
    58→- QMD enrichment happens in the next step (step-04-enrich)
    59→- Extraction results carry forward unchanged
    60→
    61→### Confidence
    62→- Extraction: same as Forge (T1)
    63→- Enrichment annotations added in step-04: T2
    64→
    65→---
    66→
    67→## Tier Degradation Rules
    68→
    69→### Remote Source at Forge/Deep Tier
    70→
    71→When `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) and the tier is Forge or Deep:
    72→
    73→- **ast-grep requires local files** — it cannot operate on remote URLs
    74→- The extraction step MUST warn the user explicitly before degrading
    75→- Warning must include actionable guidance: clone locally and update `source_repo`
    76→- Extraction proceeds using Quick tier strategy (source reading via gh_bridge)
    77→- All results labeled T1-low with `[SRC:...]` citations
    78→- The degradation reason is recorded in the evidence report
    79→
    80→Silent degradation is **forbidden**. The user must always know when AST extraction was skipped and why.
    81→
    82→### AST Tool Unavailable at Forge/Deep Tier
    83→
    84→When the tier is Forge or Deep but ast-grep is not functional:
    85→
    86→- The extraction step MUST warn the user explicitly before degrading
    87→- Warning must include actionable guidance: run [SF] Setup Forge to detect tools
    88→- Extraction proceeds using Quick tier strategy
    89→- All results labeled T1-low
    90→- The degradation reason is recorded in the evidence report
    91→
    92→### Per-File AST Failure
    93→
    94→When ast-grep fails on an individual file (parse error, unsupported syntax):
    95→
    96→- Fall back to source reading for **that file only**
    97→- Other files continue with AST extraction
    98→- The affected file's results are labeled T1-low; unaffected files retain T1
    99→- Log a warning noting which file degraded and why
   100→
```

  "new_string": "### Remote Source at Forge/Deep Tier\n\nWhen `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) and the tier is Forge or Deep:\n\n- **ast-grep requires local files** — it cannot operate on remote URLs\n\n**Ephemeral clone strategy (preferred):**\n\n1. Check `git` availability (`git --version`). `git` is effectively guaranteed at Deep tier (via `gh` dependency) but NOT guaranteed at Forge tier.\n2. If `git` is available: perform an ephemeral shallow clone to a system temp path (`{system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/`).\n3. For create-skill: use `--depth 1 --single-branch`; apply sparse-checkout if `include_patterns` are specified.\n4. For update-skill: use sparse-checkout scoped to the changed files from the change manifest only.\n5. If clone succeeds: use the local clone path for AST extraction. All results are T1 with `[AST:...]` citations.\n6. Cleanup: delete the temp directory after extraction inventory is built and all data is in context. The clone never persists beyond the extraction step.\n\n**Fallback (clone fails or `git` unavailable):**\n\n- The extraction step MUST warn the user explicitly before degrading\n- Warning must include actionable guidance: clone locally and update `source_repo` (or re-run [CS] Create Skill for update-skill)\n- Extraction proceeds using Quick tier strategy (source reading via gh_bridge)\n- All results labeled T1-low with `[SRC:...]` citations\n- The degradation reason is recorded in the evidence report\n\nSilent degradation is **forbidden**. The user must always know when AST extraction was skipped and why."
}

**Here's what changed across all three files:**

---

### File 1: `create-skill/step-03-extract.md`

**Before:** Remote Source Resolution Check warned immediately and degraded to Quick tier.

**After:** Full ephemeral clone strategy:
1. Check `git` availability
2. Shallow clone to `{system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/`
3. Sparse-checkout if `include_patterns` specified
4. Clone succeeds → AST extraction on local clone (T1)
5. Clone fails → Option B warning + Quick tier fallback (T1-low)
6. Cleanup after Section 4 (Build Extraction Inventory)

---

### File 2: `update-skill/step-03-re-extract.md`

**Before:** Remote Source Resolution Check warned and overrode to Quick tier.

**After:** Same ephemeral clone pattern but with **sparse-checkout scoped to changed files** from step-02's change manifest — avoids full repo download when only 3 files changed. Cleanup after Section 4 (Compile Extraction Results).

---

### File 3: `extraction-patterns.md`

**Before:** "Remote Source at Forge/Deep Tier" section only documented the warn-and-degrade path.

**After:** Two-phase strategy:
- **Preferred:** Ephemeral clone with `git` availability gate, temp path lifecycle, sparse-checkout rules per workflow
- **Fallback:** Explicit warning + Quick tier (unchanged from Option B)

Plus notes on `git` availability: guaranteed at Deep tier (via `gh`), NOT guaranteed at Forge tier.
