## Review: Ephemeral Cloning Support for AST Extraction

Reviewing three edited files against two reference files and the surrounding workflow context.

---

## CRITICAL Issues

### Issue 1 — `{branch}` is undefined in update-skill's clone command (Confidence: 95)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`, line 72

**Problem:** The git clone command uses `{branch}`, but the update-skill workflow never establishes what `{branch}` is. The update-skill init step (`step-01-init.md`) extracts `name`, `skill_type`, `version`, `generation_date`, `confidence_tier`, and `source_root` from `metadata.json` — no `branch` field. The provenance map schema (from `provenance-tracking.md`) also records no branch. The update-skill's `step-02-detect-changes.md` never mentions branch resolution anywhere.

By contrast, create-skill correctly resolves `{branch}` in `step-01-load-brief.md` (via `gh_bridge.list_tree`, detecting the default branch) and stores it in context. That resolution step has no equivalent in the update-skill flow.

In practice, a model executing this step will either hallucinate a branch name (violating zero-hallucination) or produce a broken `git clone` command.

**Fix:** Either (a) add branch resolution to update-skill step-01 (read `source_branch` from `metadata.json` or provenance map, fall back to detecting the default branch via `gh_bridge`), or (b) drop `--branch {branch}` from the clone command and rely on `--single-branch` against the remote default, noting that this must match the branch used during create-skill. Also update `metadata.json` generation in create-skill step-07 to persist the resolved branch alongside `source_repo`.

---

### Issue 2 — `{source_root_url}` is a new alias used only once, inconsistently with the variable name in scope (Confidence: 88)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`, line 72

**Problem:** The surrounding prose correctly uses `{source_root}` (from `metadata.json`) throughout — the condition check on line 62 (`If source_root is a local path`), the warning message on line 84 (`Ephemeral clone of '{source_root}' failed`), and `extraction-patterns.md` line 87 (`update source_repo` in the fallback guidance). But the git command itself introduces `{source_root_url}` — a new alias defined nowhere in the step or in any previous step's output. A model executing this step may treat it as a distinct, unresolved variable.

In create-skill the analogous alias `{source_repo_url}` is equally undocumented, but there `source_repo` is a required brief field that is always a URL or owner/repo string, so the alias is at least a plausible inference. For update-skill, `source_root` could historically be a local path (the local-path branch is already handled above in the same section), so the rename is both ambiguous and inconsistent.

**Fix:** Replace `{source_root_url}` with `{source_root}` on line 72 and add one sentence clarifying that `{source_root}` at this point in the branch is known to be a remote URL (since the local-path case was already returned above).

---

### Issue 3 — Cleanup is placed after Section 4, but cleanup is NOT guaranteed if the step errors between clone success and Section 4 completion (Confidence: 85)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`, line 103; same issue in `step-03-re-extract.md`, line 88

**Problem:** Both files describe cleanup as: "After Section 4 completes — when all extracted data is in context — if `ephemeral_clone_active`, delete the `{temp_path}` directory."

This wording makes cleanup contingent on Section 4 completing successfully. If extraction fails part-way through (e.g., AST tool crashes mid-scan, context window limit hit, subprocess unresponsive), Section 4 never completes, `ephemeral_clone_active` is never cleared, and the temp directory is never deleted. The system temp will accumulate `skf-ephemeral-*` directories from every failed run.

The individual-file failure handling (line 129–131 in create-skill step-03) correctly says "skip and continue," so partial extraction failures won't block Section 4 — but a catastrophic failure (crash, kill) will. Given these are AI-agent workflows where context exhaustion is a realistic failure mode, this is not a theoretical concern.

**Fix:** Reframe cleanup as: "After extraction is complete for all files in scope (whether successful or partially failed), before presenting Gate 2, if `ephemeral_clone_active`, delete `{temp_path}`." Move it to just before Section 5 (Gate 2 summary) rather than "after Section 4." This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still running.

---

## Important Issues

### Issue 4 — `extraction-patterns.md` fallback guidance uses `source_repo` when update-skill users must use a different action (Confidence: 90)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`, lines 87–88

**Problem:** The fallback text in the "Tier Degradation Rules" section says:

> "Warning must include actionable guidance: clone locally and update `source_repo` (or re-run [CS] Create Skill for update-skill)"

This is the canonical description that both workflows reference for their fallback behavior. But update-skill step-03-re-extract.md line 84 correctly implements a different message:

> "clone the repository locally and re-run [CS] Create Skill with the local path, then re-run this update."

The extraction-patterns.md text parenthetical `(or re-run [CS] Create Skill for update-skill)` reads as a second option rather than the required action. A model reading this fragment as context might implement the warning as "update `source_repo`" (incorrect for update-skill — the user needs to re-run create-skill with a local path, not just edit a field). The step file itself is correct; the pattern doc is ambiguous.

**Fix:** Separate the two cases clearly in extraction-patterns.md:
- create-skill: "clone locally and update `source_repo` in the brief to the local path"
- update-skill: "clone locally, re-run [CS] Create Skill with the local path to generate provenance data, then re-run this update"

---

### Issue 5 — `progressive-capability.md` still describes the old Option B (warn-only) behavior as the complete strategy; the ephemeral clone path is not reflected (Confidence: 90)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/progressive-capability.md`, lines 107–111

**Problem:** The "Graceful Degradation" section, which is the canonical reference for how remote source + Forge/Deep tier is handled, still reads entirely as the pre-ephemeral Option B:

> "When source is remote (GitHub URL) and tier is Forge or Deep:
> 1. AST extraction requires local files — ast-grep cannot operate on remote URLs
> 2. The extraction step warns the user explicitly before degrading to source reading (T1-low)
> 3. The warning includes actionable guidance: clone locally and update `source_repo` to a local path
> 4. Silent degradation is forbidden"

This description is now incorrect as the primary path. The ephemeral clone is attempted first; warning + degradation is the fallback. Any model or developer reading `progressive-capability.md` as authoritative will implement the old behavior, missing the ephemeral clone attempt entirely.

The Anti-Patterns section on line 119 also still lists only the silent-degradation anti-pattern, not missing the ephemeral clone attempt.

**Fix:** Update the Graceful Degradation section to describe the three-phase strategy: (1) attempt ephemeral shallow clone, (2) if clone fails, warn and degrade, (3) silent degradation forbidden. Update Example 3 (Tier-Adaptive Extraction) to mention the ephemeral clone path alongside the local-source path.

---

### Issue 6 — The two-step sparse-checkout in create-skill uses `--filter=blob:none` but the full-clone path does not; the two paths can produce different working trees (Confidence: 82)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`, lines 82–90

**Problem:** When `include_patterns` are NOT specified, the full clone command is:
```
git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch {source_repo_url} {temp_path}
```

When `include_patterns` ARE specified, the sparse clone command is:
```
git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo_url} {temp_path}
git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {include_patterns}
```

`--filter=blob:none` in the sparse path means blobs are fetched lazily — only when accessed. `sparse-checkout set` then restricts the working tree. But on the non-sparse path, all blobs are fetched for the entire repository. For large repositories this is intentionally avoided by the sparse path, so the full path could time out or exhaust memory, undermining the "ephemeral" design goal. More subtly, `extraction-patterns.md` line 79 says "use `--depth 1 --single-branch`" for create-skill with no mention of `--filter`, implying the full clone is expected — but this conflicts with the intent to be lightweight.

This is not a correctness bug (both clones are valid git operations) but it is an inconsistency that could lead to very slow or failed clones for large repos without `include_patterns`, defeating the purpose of the ephemeral strategy.

**Fix:** Either (a) apply `--filter=blob:none` to the full-clone path as well (making the non-sparse path `git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo_url} {temp_path}`) or (b) document explicitly in extraction-patterns.md that the full clone path is for smaller repositories and recommend always specifying `include_patterns` for large repos.

---

## Low-Confidence / Observations (below threshold, noted for completeness)

**Observation A — `{source_repo_url}` alias in create-skill is also undocumented** (same issue as Issue 2, lower severity because `source_repo` is always URL-or-owner/repo in a brief). Confidence 70 — does not meet the 80 threshold because the inference is reasonable.

**Observation B — Security: `{source_repo_url}` and `{source_root_url}` are user-controlled strings inserted into a shell command.** In an AI-agent context where the model constructs and executes the git command, a crafted value like `https://example.com --upload-pack=malicious` could inject git flags. Confidence 65 — these are AI-authored workflows where the "shell" is conceptual, not a real shell, and the model is expected to validate URLs. Below threshold but worth noting if these patterns ever drive actual subprocess execution.

**Observation C — `create-stack-skill/step-04-parallel-extract.md` correctly excludes ephemeral clone.** Line 57 explicitly states "Remote source resolution does not apply — libraries are analyzed as they exist within the project's dependency tree." This is accurate and consistent with the design. No action needed.

---
